Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Linea Directa Aseguradora (LDA-ES), a Spanish pure direct-to-consumer personal-lines insurer selling through telephone, web and app from a single site in Tres Cantos, Madrid, with no branches or brokers and its own repair centres (CAR); segments are motor (about 81% of gross written premiums), home (about 15%), health (about 3%, formerly the Vivaz brand, 50% ceded to Munich Re under a quota share to December 2029) and small pet, SME and mobility lines; Spain is over 99.9% of premiums.

Request: OWN-1

Question: What do the published research notes on Linea Directa Aseguradora (LDA-ES) from the brokers that cover it (UBS, JB Capital Markets, Bestinver Securities, Renta 4, Santander, CaixaBank BPI, Alantra and any other house) say since the Q3 2026 results of 28-Oct-26 (nine-month net profit EUR 80.4m, combined ratio 91.3%, gross written premiums +8.9%, solvency 191.5%), and what did they say after the H1 2026 results of 27-Jul-26: for each house, its rating, target price and FY2026 to FY2028 estimates for premiums, combined ratio by segment, net profit, EPS and dividend per share, and its stated view on (a) the motor pricing of about 2% on new business and renewals against a market of about 6% that the CFO cited on the Q1 2026 call of 27-Apr-26 and its effect on 2027 earned margins, (b) the 2027 Baremo update (the pension revaluation formula; the 3.33% preliminary estimate reported on 3-Nov-26), (c) Mutua Madrileña's tariff freeze for 50% of its auto customers and 97% combined-ratio ceiling, (d) the home combined ratio against the "normal" 90% to 91% the CFO described on 23-Feb-26, (e) the dividend and any buyback after the Solvency II revision of 30-Jan-27, and (f) the Bankinter 17.42% stake; and which of these items each house models explicitly rather than mentions?

Request: OWN-2

Question: What do Linea Directa Aseguradora's (LDA-ES) published Solvency and Financial Condition Reports and quantitative reporting templates for 2021 to 2025 (in particular the claims development triangles, template S.19.01), its individual and consolidated annual accounts for 2023 to 2025 (which disclose a prior-year run-off result for motor third-party liability, cited in research as a EUR 15.92m surplus and a EUR 3.43m deficit for 2025) and its interim statements show, accident year by accident year, about the development of its motor bodily-injury and material-damage claims reserves, the run-off result by line and year, the share of each year's reported combined ratio that came from prior-year development, the quantified effect of raising the risk adjustment to the 95th percentile in 2023 and returning it to the 85th percentile by end-2024, the bodily-injury share of outstanding claims reserves exposed to the payment-year Baremo scale, and how these compare with the disclosed reserve releases of Mapfre Iberia (which strengthened motor reserves by 2 to 3 points of its nine-month claims ratio in Q3 2026), Grupo Catalana Occidente and Admiral Group (risk adjustment at the 94th percentile at end-2025)?

Request: OWN-3

Question: What do public tariff trackers and disclosures show about Spanish motor insurance prices written in the fourth quarter of 2026 and announced for the January 2027 renewal round, by insurer, including Linea Directa Aseguradora (LDA-ES), Mutua Madrileña (whether its tariff freeze is extended into 2027 and for what share of policyholders), Mapfre (which said on 28-Oct-26 it will move to premium and unit growth in its 2027 to 2029 plan while pricing slightly above inflation), Allianz Seguros, AXA Seguros (whose 2027 to 2029 plan of 15-Sep-26 targets more than 10% a year of direct growth in Spain), Generali and Grupo Catalana Occidente: the INE motor-insurance CPI sub-index, comparison-site price indices (Rastreator, Acierto, Kelisto), ICEA and UNESPA average premium data, consumer-association renewal surveys, insurers' own statements on new-business and renewal price changes, and expert or broker commentary on whether direct insurers are raising renewal prices into 2027; and what Spanish motor claims-cost indicators (the Jefferies claims-cost index of spare parts +5.1% and repair and maintenance +3.7% year on year in September 2026; CESVIMAP, TIREA and workshop-association data) show for the same period?
